and lenders ought to be listening to me instead of doubting me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n Well, it's a mix, I guess. Some mornings during those high-gear times, yeah, I feel like the whole valley is just opening up for me. I'll be out there on the tractor grinning at absolutely nothing. 

But... it's not always just happy. If one of the hired hands questions how I'm doing things, or if my wife tells me I need to slow down, I get real short. I'll snap at 'em like they just insulted the family name. So, I feel real good, but I also get pretty fired up if anyone tries to get in my way or slow me down.

I don't think so. I haven't really been feeling down or depressed. Like I mentioned, it's mostly just been that high gear, or just regular days working the farm. I haven't really had any low spells to speak of. So, no, I haven't heard them during a time like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt really down or depressed and also heard the voices in the irrigation lines at the same time?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that, doc. Like I said a second ago... no. I haven't had any periods where I felt down or depressed at all. It's just been those high-gear times or regular days working the farm. So I haven't heard them while feeling low, because I just haven't been feeling low.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling really energetic and driven, does it always start and stop abruptly, or is it more of a gradual change?\n\nPatient: It's... well, it's pretty sudden, usually. One day I'm just going about my business, getting the chores done, and then... bam. It's like someone flipped a switch on a tractor. I just wake up and I'm running on all cylinders, ready to take on the whole farm before noon. And when it stops, it's kind of the same way. The engine just cuts out, and I'm back to my regular speed. It doesn't really build up slow or fade out, it just happens.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling really energetic and driven, do you always feel extremely happy or like you're on top of the world at the same time?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix, I guess. Some mornings during those high-gear times, yeah, I feel like the whole valley is just opening up for me. I'll be out there on the tractor grinning at absolutely nothing. \n\nBut... it's not always just happy. If one of the hired hands questions how I'm doing things, or if my wife tells me I need to slow down, I get real short. I'll snap at 'em like they just insulted the family name. So, I feel real good, but I also get pretty fired up if anyone tries to get in my way or slow me down.\n\nDoctor: After one of these high-energy periods, do you usually feel tired, empty, or depressed afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I do. Like I mentioned a few minutes ago, I don't really get those down or depressed feelings. \n\nWhen that high energy stops, I just... well, I just go back to my normal speed. I mean, my body might be a regular kind of tired if I've been running a tractor for fourteen hours straight, but mentally? No, I don't feel empty or low. I just go back to feeling like my regular self, doing my regular chores.\n\nDoctor: How long has the longest period of feeling really energetic and driven lasted for you?\n\nPatient: Well, if I look back over the past year or so... it's been most of it. I'd say those charged-up, nonstop stretches where I hardly need any sleep have taken up about ten months out of the last twelve. It just keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt really down or depressed, and if so, how long did it last?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. Like I said before, I don't get those down spells. I don't feel sad or empty or anything like that. I just have my normal days, and then those long stretches where I'm fired up and running on all cylinders. But no, no periods of feeling depressed at all.\n\nDoctor: How many days in a row have you typically felt this extremely energetic and driven during one of these high-gear times?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't exactly count the days. Like I was saying a minute ago, it just... it stretches on for months at a time. I couldn't give you a specific number of days in a row. It just feels like one long, continuous run where I'm up and working the farm, day after day, until it finally stops. I just lose track of the exact count."
